+package api
+
+import (
+	"crypto/rand"
+	"crypto/rsa"
+	"encoding/base64"
+	"encoding/json"
+	"net/http"
+	"net/http/httptest"
+	"testing"
+	"time"
+)
+
+func jwkFromPublicKey(kid string, pub *rsa.PublicKey) jwk {
+	return jwk{
+		Kty: "RSA",
+		Kid: kid,
+		Alg: "RS256",
+		Use: "sig",
+		N:   base64.RawURLEncoding.EncodeToString(pub.N.Bytes()),
+		E:   base64.RawURLEncoding.EncodeToString(bigEndianExponent(pub.E)),
+	}
+}
+
+func bigEndianExponent(e int) []byte {
+	// Standard RSA public exponent (65537) fits in 3 bytes; encode
+	// minimally, matching how real JWKS producers encode "e".
+	b := []byte{byte(e >> 16), byte(e >> 8), byte(e)}
+	i := 0
+	for i < len(b)-1 && b[i] == 0 {
+		i++
+	}
+	return b[i:]
+}
+
+func TestParseJWKS_RoundTrip(t *testing.T) {
+	key, err := rsa.GenerateKey(rand.Reader, 2048)
+	if err != nil {
+		t.Fatalf("generating key: %v", err)
+	}
+	doc := jwksResponse{Keys: []jwk{jwkFromPublicKey("key-1", &key.PublicKey)}}
+	data, err := json.Marshal(doc)
+	if err != nil {
+		t.Fatalf("marshaling JWKS: %v", err)
+	}
+
+	keys, err := parseJWKS(data)
+	if err != nil {
+		t.Fatalf("parseJWKS: %v", err)
+	}
+	got, ok := keys["key-1"]
+	if !ok {
+		t.Fatal("parsed key set missing key-1")
+	}
+	if got.N.Cmp(key.PublicKey.N) != 0 || got.E != key.PublicKey.E {
+		t.Error("parsed public key does not match the original")
+	}
+}
+
+func TestParseJWKS_SkipsNonRSAKeys(t *testing.T) {
+	data := []byte(`{"keys":[{"kty":"EC","kid":"ec-1","crv":"P-256","x":"AAAA","y":"AAAA"}]}`)
+	keys, err := parseJWKS(data)
+	if err != nil {
+		t.Fatalf("parseJWKS: %v", err)
+	}
+	if len(keys) != 0 {
+		t.Errorf("expected a non-RSA key to be skipped, got %d keys", len(keys))
+	}
+}
+
+func TestParseJWKS_MissingKidIsError(t *testing.T) {
+	data := []byte(`{"keys":[{"kty":"RSA","n":"AAAA","e":"AQAB"}]}`)
+	if _, err := parseJWKS(data); err == nil {
+		t.Error("expected an error for an RSA key with no kid")
+	}
+}

+func TestJWKSCache_FetchesAndCaches(t *testing.T) {
+	key, err := rsa.GenerateKey(rand.Reader, 2048)
+	if err != nil {
+		t.Fatalf("generating key: %v", err)
+	}
+	doc := jwksResponse{Keys: []jwk{jwkFromPublicKey("key-1", &key.PublicKey)}}
+	body, err := json.Marshal(doc)
+	if err != nil {
+		t.Fatalf("marshaling JWKS: %v", err)
+	}
+
+	requests := 0
+	srv := httptest.NewServer(http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
+		requests++
+		w.Header().Set("Content-Type", "application/json")
+		_, _ = w.Write(body)
+	}))
+	defer srv.Close()
+
+	cache := NewJWKSCache(srv.URL, time.Hour, nil)
+	if _, err := cache.KeyForKid("key-1"); err != nil {
+		t.Fatalf("first KeyForKid: %v", err)
+	}
+	if _, err := cache.KeyForKid("key-1"); err != nil {
+		t.Fatalf("second KeyForKid: %v", err)
+	}
+	if requests != 1 {
+		t.Errorf("fetched the JWKS endpoint %d times, want exactly 1 (cached within TTL)", requests)
+	}
+}

+func TestJWKSCache_RefetchesAfterTTLExpires(t *testing.T) {
+	key, err := rsa.GenerateKey(rand.Reader, 2048)
+	if err != nil {
+		t.Fatalf("generating key: %v", err)
+	}
+	doc := jwksResponse{Keys: []jwk{jwkFromPublicKey("key-1", &key.PublicKey)}}
+	body, err := json.Marshal(doc)
+	if err != nil {
+		t.Fatalf("marshaling JWKS: %v", err)
+	}
+
+	requests := 0
+	srv := httptest.NewServer(http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
+		requests++
+		w.Header().Set("Content-Type", "application/json")
+		_, _ = w.Write(body)
+	}))
+	defer srv.Close()
+
+	cache := NewJWKSCache(srv.URL, time.Millisecond, nil)
+	if _, err := cache.KeyForKid("key-1"); err != nil {
+		t.Fatalf("first KeyForKid: %v", err)
+	}
+	time.Sleep(5 * time.Millisecond)
+	if _, err := cache.KeyForKid("key-1"); err != nil {
+		t.Fatalf("second KeyForKid: %v", err)
+	}
+	if requests < 2 {
+		t.Errorf("fetched the JWKS endpoint %d times, want at least 2 (TTL should have expired)", requests)
+	}
+}

+func TestJWKSCache_UnknownKidIsError(t *testing.T) {

+	}))
+	defer srv.Close()

+func TestJWKSCache_StaleCacheSurvivesTransientFetchFailure(t *testing.T) {
+	key, err := rsa.GenerateKey(rand.Reader, 2048)
+	if err != nil {
+		t.Fatalf("generating key: %v", err)
+	}
+	doc := jwksResponse{Keys: []jwk{jwkFromPublicKey("key-1", &key.PublicKey)}}
+	body, err := json.Marshal(doc)
+	if err != nil {
+		t.Fatalf("marshaling JWKS: %v", err)
+	}
+
+	failing := false
+	srv := httptest.NewServer(http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
+		if failing {
+			w.WriteHeader(http.StatusInternalServerError)
+			return
+		}
+		w.Header().Set("Content-Type", "application/json")
+		_, _ = w.Write(body)
+	}))
+	defer srv.Close()
+
+	cache := NewJWKSCache(srv.URL, time.Millisecond, nil)
+	if _, err := cache.KeyForKid("key-1"); err != nil {
+		t.Fatalf("first KeyForKid: %v", err)
+	}
+	time.Sleep(5 * time.Millisecond)
+	failing = true
+	if _, err := cache.KeyForKid("key-1"); err != nil {
+		t.Errorf("KeyForKid during a transient fetch failure = %v, want the stale cache to still serve the key", err)
+	}
+}

+// jwtmiddleware.go is LT-40's core: verify the client-credentials JWT
+// scheme from handoff-03-auth.md v3/v4 and populate AuthContext for
+// LT-39's already-built handlers. This is the actual integration seam
+// with S6's work, not new response-shape design — a verification failure
+// uses the exact same writeUnauthorized this package's handlers already
+// call for a missing AuthContext.
+package api
+
+import (
+	"context"
+	"errors"
+	"net/http"
+	"strings"
+	"time"
+
+	"github.com/golang-jwt/jwt/v5"
+)
+
+// jwtAlgorithm is the one signing algorithm this verifier ever accepts.
+// Passed explicitly to jwt.WithValidMethods on every parse — never left
+// to the library's default, per handoff-03-auth.md v3's explicit
+// instruction ("check your JWT library's default behavior — several
+// popular libraries have shipped this vulnerability by not doing this by
+// default") and RFC 8725 §3.1. This is what makes alg:none and an
+// HMAC-with-the-public-key-as-secret downgrade both fail before the
+// library ever calls KeySource — jwt/v5's WithValidMethods rejects a
+// token whose header alg isn't in this allowlist ahead of signature
+// verification, not as a secondary check after.
+const jwtAlgorithm = "RS256"
+
+// claims is this service's JWT claim set (handoff-03-auth.md v3's
+// minimum: sub, scope, exp, iat, aud, plus iss and kid via the header).
+// RegisteredClaims gives exp/iat/iss/aud validation for free from the
+// library — this verifier still explicitly re-checks Audience below
+// because jwt/v5's built-in audience check (via WithAudience) does not
+// distinguish "no aud claim at all" from "aud present but empty," and a
+// token with no audience claim must not silently pass as if it matched.
+type claims struct {
+	Scope string `json:"scope"`
+	jwt.RegisteredClaims
+}
+
+// jwtVerifier is the collaborator NewJWTMiddleware wraps — an interface
+// so a test can supply a fake instead of a real *JWKSCache doing network
+// I/O, mirroring this package's existing test-double approach
+// (decisions/test-double-strategy.md).
+type jwtVerifier struct {
+	keys     KeySource
+	issuer   string
+	audience string
+}
+
+// verify parses and fully validates tokenString, returning the populated
+// AuthContext on success. Every failure returns a generic error — the
+// specific reason (expired, wrong audience, bad signature, unknown kid,
+// disallowed algorithm) is never distinguishable to the caller of this
+// function beyond "verification failed," which is what makes
+// writeUnauthorized's single, undifferentiated 401 body correct here
+// without an extra translation step.
+func (v *jwtVerifier) verify(tokenString string) (AuthContext, error) {
+	var c claims
+	token, err := jwt.ParseWithClaims(tokenString, &c, func(t *jwt.Token) (any, error) {
+		kid, _ := t.Header["kid"].(string)
+		if kid == "" {
+			return nil, errors.New("token header has no kid")
+		}
+		return v.keys.KeyForKid(kid)
+	}, jwt.WithValidMethods([]string{jwtAlgorithm}), jwt.WithIssuer(v.issuer))
+	if err != nil {
+		return AuthContext{}, err
+	}
+	if !token.Valid {
+		return AuthContext{}, errors.New("token invalid")
+	}
+
+	// Audience checked explicitly, not via jwt.WithAudience above: v5's
+	// audience option treats an all-nil/absent Audience claim
+	// inconsistently across versions in a way we don't want to depend
+	// on — asserting it here directly means this check's behavior is
+	// this file's own to guarantee, not the library's default to trust.
+	if !hasAudience(c.RegisteredClaims.Audience, v.audience) {
+		return AuthContext{}, errors.New("token audience mismatch")
+	}
+
+	if c.Subject == "" {
+		return AuthContext{}, errors.New("token has no sub claim")
+	}
+
+	scope, err := singleScope(c.Scope)
+	if err != nil {
+		return AuthContext{}, err
+	}
+
+	return AuthContext{Sub: c.Subject, Scope: scope}, nil
+}
+
+func hasAudience(aud jwt.ClaimStrings, want string) bool {
+	for _, a := range aud {
+		if a == want {
+			return true
+		}
+	}
+	return false
+}
+
+// singleScope requires the token's scope claim to name exactly one
+// recognized scope. handoff-03-auth.md v3 describes the claim as
+// "space-delimited, values below" without describing how a handler
+// disambiguates a multi-scope token per request, and AuthContext (LT-39)
+// carries exactly one Scope by design ("handoff-03-auth.md v3 doesn't
+// describe multi-scope tokens being disambiguated per-request, so one
+// request carries exactly one Scope"). Rather than guess which of
+// several granted scopes a request is "really" using — silently picking
+// one is exactly the kind of authorization ambiguity this project's
+// contracts have consistently ruled against — a token naming zero or
+// more than one recognized scope is rejected outright. This matches the
+// provisioning model in decisions/search-authz-scoping.md: a client is
+// provisioned for the one use case its credential exists for.
+func singleScope(raw string) (Scope, error) {
+	fields := strings.Fields(raw)
+	var found []Scope
+	for _, f := range fields {
+		switch Scope(f) {
+		case ScopeReadOwn, ScopeReadAny, ScopeSearch:
+			found = append(found, Scope(f))
+		}
+	}
+	switch len(found) {
+	case 0:
+		return "", errors.New("token scope claim names no recognized scope")
+	case 1:
+		return found[0], nil
+	default:
+		return "", errors.New("token scope claim names more than one recognized scope, which this verifier does not disambiguate per request")
+	}
+}
+
+// NewJWTMiddleware verifies every request's bearer token and populates
+// AuthContext on success, per handoff-03-auth.md v3/v4: public-key-only
+// verification (keys never includes signing-key material — enforced
+// structurally, since KeySource's real implementation, JWKSCache, only
+// ever holds *rsa.PublicKey values parsed from a JWKS response), pinned
+// RS256, kid-based key selection supporting a rotation overlap window (a
+// KeySource backed by the issuer's full published key set, not a single
+// cached key), and issuer/audience checks. Verification failure returns
+// 401 via writeUnauthorized — the same path LT-39's handlers already use
+// for a missing AuthContext, not a new response shape.
+func NewJWTMiddleware(keys KeySource, issuer, audience string) func(http.Handler) http.Handler {
+	v := &jwtVerifier{keys: keys, issuer: issuer, audience: audience}
+	return func(next http.Handler) http.Handler {
+		return http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
+			tokenString, ok := bearerToken(r)
+			if !ok {
+				writeUnauthorized(w)
+				return
+			}
+			ac, err := v.verify(tokenString)
+			if err != nil {
+				writeUnauthorized(w)
+				return
+			}
+			r = r.WithContext(WithAuthContext(r.Context(), ac))
+			next.ServeHTTP(w, r)
+		})
+	}
+}
+
+func bearerToken(r *http.Request) (string, bool) {
+	h := r.Header.Get("Authorization")
+	const prefix = "Bearer "
+	if !strings.HasPrefix(h, prefix) {
+		return "", false
+	}
+	tok := strings.TrimSpace(strings.TrimPrefix(h, prefix))
+	if tok == "" {
+		return "", false
+	}
+	return tok, true
+}
+
+// deadlineBudget is the context.WithTimeout duration this middleware
+// applies to every request before it reaches a handler — the concrete
+// number decisions/context-propagation.md's ruling required ("this gets
+// a concrete number attached when [the middleware] is written, not left
+// as 'some deadline'"). Sized to cover realistic p99 DAO latency
+// (single-digit milliseconds for an indexed Postgres/CockroachDB point
+// query or trigram search, per 05-data-ops/research-cockroachdb-postgres-semantics.md)
+// plus this middleware's own JWKS verification path (a cache hit is
+// sub-millisecond; a cache-miss fetch to the issuer's in-cluster Service
+// is bounded by deadlineBudget itself, not a separate timeout, since it
+// happens inside the same request). 5 seconds leaves an order of
+// magnitude of headroom over expected p99 without ever handing the DAO
+// layer a near-zero remaining deadline as a matter of routine operation.
+const deadlineBudget = 5 * time.Second
+
+// NewDeadlineMiddleware applies deadlineBudget to every request's
+// context before it reaches a handler — the enforcement site
+// decisions/context-propagation.md's ruling named ("a concrete number
+// attached when [the middleware] is written"). Composed outermost (see
+// router wiring), so the budget covers JWT verification (including a
+// JWKS cache-miss fetch) and the handler's own DAO call under one
+// deadline, not two independently-sized timeouts that could disagree.
+func NewDeadlineMiddleware(next http.Handler) http.Handler {
+	return http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
+		ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(r.Context(), deadlineBudget)
+		defer cancel()
+		next.ServeHTTP(w, r.WithContext(ctx))
+	})
+}

+
+	"github.com/golang-jwt/jwt/v5"

+const (
+	testIssuer   = "https://auth.loginid-takehome.internal"
+	testAudience = "loginid-api-service"
+)
+
+// fakeKeySource implements KeySource with an in-memory map, for tests
+// that don't need real JWKS-over-HTTP fetching (that's jwks_test.go's
+// job).
+type fakeKeySource struct {
+	keys map[string]*rsa.PublicKey
+}
+
+func (f *fakeKeySource) KeyForKid(kid string) (*rsa.PublicKey, error) {
+	k, ok := f.keys[kid]
+	if !ok {
+		return nil, errNoKey
+	}
+	return k, nil
+}
+
+var errNoKey = jwt.ErrTokenUnverifiable
+
+func generateRSAKeyPair(t *testing.T) *rsa.PrivateKey {
+	t.Helper()
+	key, err := rsa.GenerateKey(rand.Reader, 2048)
+	if err != nil {
+		t.Fatalf("generating RSA key: %v", err)
+	}
+	return key
+}
+
+type tokenOpts struct {
+	kid      string
+	alg      jwt.SigningMethod
+	issuer   string
+	audience string
+	subject  string
+	scope    string
+	expired  bool
+	notYet   bool
+}
+
+func signToken(t *testing.T, key *rsa.PrivateKey, opts tokenOpts) string {
+	t.Helper()
+	now := time.Now()
+	exp := now.Add(10 * time.Minute)
+	if opts.expired {
+		exp = now.Add(-1 * time.Minute)
+	}
+	iat := now
+	nbf := now
+	if opts.notYet {
+		nbf = now.Add(1 * time.Hour)
+	}
+	c := claims{
+		Scope: opts.scope,
+		RegisteredClaims: jwt.RegisteredClaims{
+			Issuer:    opts.issuer,
+			Subject:   opts.subject,
+			Audience:  jwt.ClaimStrings{opts.audience},
+			ExpiresAt: jwt.NewNumericDate(exp),
+			IssuedAt:  jwt.NewNumericDate(iat),
+			NotBefore: jwt.NewNumericDate(nbf),
+		},
+	}
+	token := jwt.NewWithClaims(opts.alg, c)
+	token.Header["kid"] = opts.kid
+	signed, err := token.SignedString(key)
+	if err != nil {
+		t.Fatalf("signing token: %v", err)
+	}
+	return signed
+}
+
+func defaultOpts(kid string) tokenOpts {
+	return tokenOpts{
+		kid:      kid,
+		alg:      jwt.SigningMethodRS256,
+		issuer:   testIssuer,
+		audience: testAudience,
+		subject:  "client-under-test",
+		scope:    string(ScopeSearch),
+	}
+}
+
+func newTestMiddleware(keys map[string]*rsa.PublicKey) func(http.Handler) http.Handler {
+	return NewJWTMiddleware(&fakeKeySource{keys: keys}, testIssuer, testAudience)
+}
+
+func okHandler(gotCtx *AuthContext) http.Handler {
+	return http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
+		if ac, ok := AuthContextFromContext(r.Context()); ok {
+			*gotCtx = ac
+		}
+		w.WriteHeader(http.StatusOK)
+	})
+}
+
+func doRequest(mw func(http.Handler) http.Handler, token string) (*httptest.ResponseRecorder, AuthContext) {
+	var gotCtx AuthContext
+	h := mw(okHandler(&gotCtx))
+	req := httptest.NewRequest(http.MethodGet, "/profiles/search", nil)
+	if token != "" {
+		req.Header.Set("Authorization", "Bearer "+token)
+	}
+	w := httptest.NewRecorder()
+	h.ServeHTTP(w, req)
+	return w, gotCtx
+}
+
+func TestJWTMiddleware_ValidTokenPopulatesAuthContext(t *testing.T) {
+	key := generateRSAKeyPair(t)
+	mw := newTestMiddleware(map[string]*rsa.PublicKey{"key-1": &key.PublicKey})
+	tok := signToken(t, key, defaultOpts("key-1"))
+
+	w, ac := doRequest(mw, tok)
+	if w.Code != http.StatusOK {
+		t.Fatalf("status = %d, want 200", w.Code)
+	}
+	if ac.Sub != "client-under-test" || ac.Scope != ScopeSearch {
+		t.Errorf("AuthContext = %+v, want Sub=client-under-test Scope=profile:search", ac)
+	}
+}
+
+func TestJWTMiddleware_MissingToken_401(t *testing.T) {
+	key := generateRSAKeyPair(t)
+	mw := newTestMiddleware(map[string]*rsa.PublicKey{"key-1": &key.PublicKey})
+	w, _ := doRequest(mw, "")
+	if w.Code != http.StatusUnauthorized {
+		t.Errorf("status = %d, want 401", w.Code)
+	}
+}
+
+func TestJWTMiddleware_ExpiredToken_401(t *testing.T) {
+	key := generateRSAKeyPair(t)
+	mw := newTestMiddleware(map[string]*rsa.PublicKey{"key-1": &key.PublicKey})
+	opts := defaultOpts("key-1")
+	opts.expired = true
+	tok := signToken(t, key, opts)

+	w, _ := doRequest(mw, tok)
+	if w.Code != http.StatusUnauthorized {
+		t.Errorf("status = %d, want 401 for an expired token", w.Code)
+	}

+func TestJWTMiddleware_WrongAudience_401(t *testing.T) {

+	mw := newTestMiddleware(map[string]*rsa.PublicKey{"key-1": &key.PublicKey})

+	}

+func TestJWTMiddleware_WrongIssuer_401(t *testing.T) {
+	key := generateRSAKeyPair(t)
+	mw := newTestMiddleware(map[string]*rsa.PublicKey{"key-1": &key.PublicKey})
+	opts := defaultOpts("key-1")
+	opts.issuer = "https://not-the-real-issuer.example"
+	tok := signToken(t, key, opts)

+	if w.Code != http.StatusUnauthorized {
+		t.Errorf("status = %d, want 401 for a mismatched issuer", w.Code)
+	}
+}

+// TestJWTMiddleware_AlgNone_401 is RFC 8725 §3.1's classic attack: a
+// token whose header claims alg:none, carrying no signature at all. The
+// algorithm-allowlist passed to jwt.ParseWithClaims must reject this
+// before ever reaching the keyfunc/signature-verification step.
+func TestJWTMiddleware_AlgNone_401(t *testing.T) {
+	mw := newTestMiddleware(map[string]*rsa.PublicKey{})
+
+	unsignedClaims := claims{
+		Scope: string(ScopeSearch),
+		RegisteredClaims: jwt.RegisteredClaims{
+			Issuer:    testIssuer,
+			Subject:   "attacker",
+			Audience:  jwt.ClaimStrings{testAudience},
+			ExpiresAt: jwt.NewNumericDate(time.Now().Add(10 * time.Minute)),
+		},
+	}
+	tok := jwt.NewWithClaims(jwt.SigningMethodNone, unsignedClaims)
+	signed, err := tok.SignedString(jwt.UnsafeAllowNoneSignatureType)
+	if err != nil {
+		t.Fatalf("constructing alg:none token: %v", err)
+	}
+
+	w, _ := doRequest(mw, signed)
+	if w.Code != http.StatusUnauthorized {
+		t.Errorf("status = %d, want 401 for an alg:none token", w.Code)
+	}
+}
+
+// TestJWTMiddleware_HMACConfusionWithPublicKeyAsSecret_401 is RFC 8725
+// §3.1's other classic attack: an attacker who knows the RSA public key
+// (public by definition — it's published as JWKS) signs a token with
+// HS256 using the PEM-encoded public key bytes as the HMAC secret. A
+// verifier that blindly uses whatever alg the token header claims, and
+// hands the "key" returned by its keyfunc to that algorithm's verifier,
+// gets fooled: HS256 with the public key as the secret "verifies"
+// successfully against a keyfunc that returns the RSA public key
+// unconditionally. The algorithm allowlist must reject this the same way
+// it rejects alg:none.
+func TestJWTMiddleware_HMACConfusionWithPublicKeyAsSecret_401(t *testing.T) {
+	key := generateRSAKeyPair(t)
+	mw := newTestMiddleware(map[string]*rsa.PublicKey{"key-1": &key.PublicKey})
+
+	pubDER := x509.MarshalPKCS1PublicKey(&key.PublicKey)
+
+	forged := claims{
+		Scope: string(ScopeSearch),
+		RegisteredClaims: jwt.RegisteredClaims{
+			Issuer:    testIssuer,
+			Subject:   "attacker",
+			Audience:  jwt.ClaimStrings{testAudience},
+			ExpiresAt: jwt.NewNumericDate(time.Now().Add(10 * time.Minute)),
+		},
+	}
+	tok := jwt.NewWithClaims(jwt.SigningMethodHS256, forged)
+	tok.Header["kid"] = "key-1"
+	signed, err := tok.SignedString(pubDER)
+	if err != nil {
+		t.Fatalf("signing HMAC-confusion token: %v", err)
+	}
+
+	w, _ := doRequest(mw, signed)
+	if w.Code != http.StatusUnauthorized {
+		t.Errorf("status = %d, want 401 for an HS256-with-public-key-as-secret forged token", w.Code)
+	}
+}
+
+// TestJWTMiddleware_KidRotation_BothKeysVerify is the acceptance
+// criterion for key rotation: two tokens signed under two different kids
+// both verify successfully when both keys are configured, supporting a
+// rotation overlap window.
+func TestJWTMiddleware_KidRotation_BothKeysVerify(t *testing.T) {
+	oldKey := generateRSAKeyPair(t)
+	newKey := generateRSAKeyPair(t)
+	mw := newTestMiddleware(map[string]*rsa.PublicKey{
+		"key-old": &oldKey.PublicKey,
+		"key-new": &newKey.PublicKey,
+	})
+
+	oldTok := signToken(t, oldKey, defaultOpts("key-old"))
+	newTok := signToken(t, newKey, defaultOpts("key-new"))
+
+	if w, _ := doRequest(mw, oldTok); w.Code != http.StatusOK {
+		t.Errorf("old-kid token status = %d, want 200 during rotation overlap", w.Code)
+	}
+	if w, _ := doRequest(mw, newTok); w.Code != http.StatusOK {
+		t.Errorf("new-kid token status = %d, want 200 during rotation overlap", w.Code)
+	}
+}
+
+func TestJWTMiddleware_UnknownKid_401(t *testing.T) {
+	key := generateRSAKeyPair(t)
+	mw := newTestMiddleware(map[string]*rsa.PublicKey{"key-1": &key.PublicKey})
+	tok := signToken(t, key, defaultOpts("key-does-not-exist"))
+
+	w, _ := doRequest(mw, tok)
+	if w.Code != http.StatusUnauthorized {
+		t.Errorf("status = %d, want 401 for an unrecognized kid", w.Code)
+	}
+}
+
+func TestJWTMiddleware_WrongKeyForKid_401(t *testing.T) {
+	signingKey := generateRSAKeyPair(t)
+	otherKey := generateRSAKeyPair(t)
+	// The keyfunc returns otherKey's public key for "key-1" — simulating
+	// a mismatched/corrupted JWKS entry. Signature verification must fail.
+	mw := newTestMiddleware(map[string]*rsa.PublicKey{"key-1": &otherKey.PublicKey})
+	tok := signToken(t, signingKey, defaultOpts("key-1"))
+
+	w, _ := doRequest(mw, tok)
+	if w.Code != http.StatusUnauthorized {
+		t.Errorf("status = %d, want 401 when the signature doesn't verify against the returned key", w.Code)
+	}
+}
+
+func TestJWTMiddleware_NoScopeClaim_401(t *testing.T) {
+	key := generateRSAKeyPair(t)
+	mw := newTestMiddleware(map[string]*rsa.PublicKey{"key-1": &key.PublicKey})
+	opts := defaultOpts("key-1")
+	opts.scope = ""
+	tok := signToken(t, key, opts)
+
+	w, _ := doRequest(mw, tok)
+	if w.Code != http.StatusUnauthorized {
+		t.Errorf("status = %d, want 401 for a token with no recognized scope", w.Code)
+	}
+}
+
+func TestJWTMiddleware_MultipleScopes_401(t *testing.T) {
+	key := generateRSAKeyPair(t)
+	mw := newTestMiddleware(map[string]*rsa.PublicKey{"key-1": &key.PublicKey})
+	opts := defaultOpts("key-1")
+	opts.scope = string(ScopeSearch) + " " + string(ScopeReadOwn)
+	tok := signToken(t, key, opts)
+
+	w, _ := doRequest(mw, tok)
+	if w.Code != http.StatusUnauthorized {
+		t.Errorf("status = %d, want 401 — this verifier does not disambiguate a multi-scope token per request", w.Code)
+	}
+}
+
+func TestJWTMiddleware_NoSubClaim_401(t *testing.T) {
+	key := generateRSAKeyPair(t)
+	mw := newTestMiddleware(map[string]*rsa.PublicKey{"key-1": &key.PublicKey})
+	opts := defaultOpts("key-1")
+	opts.subject = ""
+	tok := signToken(t, key, opts)
+
+	w, _ := doRequest(mw, tok)
+	if w.Code != http.StatusUnauthorized {
+		t.Errorf("status = %d, want 401 for a token with no sub claim", w.Code)
+	}
+}
+
+func TestDeadlineMiddleware_AppliesDeadline(t *testing.T) {
+	var sawDeadline bool
+	h := NewDeadlineMiddleware(http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
+		_, sawDeadline = r.Context().Deadline()
+		w.WriteHeader(http.StatusOK)
+	}))
+	req := httptest.NewRequest(http.MethodGet, "/healthz", nil)
+	w := httptest.NewRecorder()
+	h.ServeHTTP(w, req)
+	if !sawDeadline {
+		t.Error("request context should carry a deadline after NewDeadlineMiddleware")
+	}
+}

+++ b/internal/api/stopgap_authorizer.go
@@ -0,0 +1,53 @@
+package api
+
+import "context"
+
+// StopgapAuthorizer is a TEMPORARY, default-deny Authorizer stand-in —
+// NOT the real caller_referral-backed policy engine (handoff-03-auth.md
+// v3's F8 resolution names that as a later story's own datastore work).
+//
+// Ruled by Marcus Ilori (02), in response to a direct question before
+// this was wired to a live public URL: "A permissive allow-all
+// Authorizer must never be deployed to a live public URL on a shared
+// production cluster, 'temporary' or not — that's the exact failure this
+// design exists to prevent, and it's a public repo, so being
+// permissive-even-briefly is a permanent public fact." Ruling: default-
+// deny, in-memory, hard-coded with a small fixed (sub, allowed record id)
+// map for the seeded QA client credential (LT-51) — enough to
+// demonstrate both the allow and deny paths of read:own's referral check
+// in the live review, without ever allowing an arbitrary caller through.
+//
+// This only affects ScopeReadOwn's referral-membership check.
+// ScopeReadAny's authorization is "has a valid reason_code," already
+// checked in the handler before Authorize() is ever called; ScopeSearch
+// has no per-record target for this Authorizer to gate at all. Both
+// return true unconditionally here — there is no real caller_referral
+// data this stopgap could consult for a real decision on those scopes
+// either way, so denying them would not be more correct, only
+// differently placeholder.
+//
+// A follow-up story replacing this with the real datastore-backed
+// implementation must exist before this is considered load-bearing
+// beyond the live-URL review — flag to the PM if it doesn't.
+type StopgapAuthorizer struct {
+	// ReadOwnAllow maps a caller sub to the set of record ids that sub
+	// may read under ScopeReadOwn — the hard-coded "referral" set this
+	// stopgap stands in for. Empty/nil means the map denies everything,
+	// which is the safe default for any sub not explicitly seeded here.
+	ReadOwnAllow map[string]map[string]bool
+}
+
+func (a *StopgapAuthorizer) Authorize(_ context.Context, sub string, scope Scope, target string) (bool, error) {
+	switch scope {
+	case ScopeReadOwn:
+		allowed, ok := a.ReadOwnAllow[sub]
+		if !ok {
+			return false, nil
+		}
+		return allowed[target], nil
+	case ScopeReadAny, ScopeSearch:
+		return true, nil
+	default:
+		return false, nil
+	}
+}
diff --git a/internal/app/verifier_router.go b/internal/app/verifier_router.go
new file mode 100644
index 0000000..647e7ab
--- /dev/null
+++ b/internal/app/verifier_router.go
@@ -0,0 +1,75 @@
+package app
+
+import (
+	"net/http"
+	"time"
+
+	"loginid-takehome/internal/api"
+	"loginid-takehome/internal/config"
+	"loginid-takehome/internal/dao"
+)
+
+// jwksCacheTTL matches the token TTL per handoff-03-auth.md v4's own
+// guidance ("Verifier caches the fetched key set in memory with a short
+// TTL (matching token TTL is reasonable)") — using the upper bound of the
+// 5-15 minute TTL range so a cache refresh never fires more often than a
+// token itself could have already expired and been re-issued.
+const jwksCacheTTL = 15 * time.Minute
+
+// NewVerifierRouter builds api-service's real, authenticated router —
+// LT-39's handlers (search/retrieve) wired behind LT-40's JWT
+// verification middleware, plus /healthz. This is the ModeVerifier
+// Deployment's router; the issuer Deployment (ModeIssuer) still uses the
+// plain NewRouter above, since it has no need for any of this — it never
+// verifies inbound tokens, only issues them.
+//
+// repo may be nil (e.g. DB_DRIVER unset in a minimal smoke-test
+// environment); the search/retrieve routes still register in that case,
+// they'll simply fail at the DAO call with whatever error a nil
+// Repository produces — this function's job is wiring, not validating
+// that a database is reachable, which is /healthz's own job (LT-52).
+func NewVerifierRouter(cfg config.Config, repo dao.Repository) http.Handler {
+	mux := http.NewServeMux()
+	mux.HandleFunc("/healthz", healthHandler("api-service"))
+
+	deps := api.Deps{
+		Repo:        repo,
+		RateLimiter: api.NewInProcessRateLimiter(),
+		TouchCounter: api.NewInProcessTouchCounter(
+			api.TouchCapPerWindow, api.TouchCapWindow),
+		AuditLog: api.StdoutAuditLogger{},
+		Authz: &api.StopgapAuthorizer{
+			ReadOwnAllow: stopgapReadOwnAllow(cfg),
+		},
+	}
+
+	keys := api.NewJWKSCache(cfg.AuthJWKSURL, jwksCacheTTL, nil)
+	authMiddleware := api.NewJWTMiddleware(keys, cfg.AuthJWTIssuer, cfg.AuthJWTAudience)
+
+	// api.NewDeadlineMiddleware outermost: the deadline budget must cover
+	// JWT verification (including a JWKS cache-miss fetch) as well as
+	// the handler's own DAO call, per decisions/context-propagation.md's
+	// ruling — a single deadline, not the middleware's and the handler's
+	// own disagreeing.
+	protect := func(h http.Handler) http.Handler {
+		return api.NewDeadlineMiddleware(authMiddleware(h))
+	}
+
+	mux.Handle("POST /profiles/search", protect(api.NewSearchHandler(deps)))
+	mux.Handle("GET /profiles/{id}", protect(api.NewGetProfileHandler(deps)))
+
+	return mux
+}
+
+// stopgapReadOwnAllow builds StopgapAuthorizer's hard-coded (sub ->
+// allowed record id) map from cfg — empty (denies everything) unless
+// both AuthzQASub and AuthzQAAllowedProfileID are set, which LT-51 does
+// once the QA client credential and a matching seeded profile exist.
+func stopgapReadOwnAllow(cfg config.Config) map[string]map[string]bool {
+	if cfg.AuthzQASub == "" || cfg.AuthzQAAllowedProfileID == "" {
+		return nil
+	}
+	return map[string]map[string]bool{
+		cfg.AuthzQASub: {cfg.AuthzQAAllowedProfileID: true},
+	}
+}

+const (
+	testIssuer   = "https://auth.loginid-takehome.internal"
+	testAudience = "loginid-api-service"

+func startFakeJWKS(t *testing.T, kid string, pub *rsa.PublicKey) *httptest.Server {
+	t.Helper()
+	body, err := json.Marshal(map[string]any{
+		"keys": []map[string]string{{
+			"kty": "RSA",
+			"kid": kid,
+			"alg": "RS256",
+			"use": "sig",
+			"n":   base64.RawURLEncoding.EncodeToString(pub.N.Bytes()),
+			"e":   base64.RawURLEncoding.EncodeToString([]byte{1, 0, 1}), // 65537
+		}},
+	})
+	if err != nil {
+		t.Fatalf("marshaling fake JWKS: %v", err)
+	}
+	return httptest.NewServer(http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
+		w.Header().Set("Content-Type", "application/json")
+		_, _ = w.Write(body)
+	}))
+}
+
+func signTestToken(t *testing.T, key *rsa.PrivateKey, kid, scope string) string {
+	t.Helper()
+	c := jwt.MapClaims{
+		"iss":   testIssuer,
+		"aud":   testAudience,
+		"sub":   "client-under-test",
+		"scope": scope,
+		"exp":   time.Now().Add(10 * time.Minute).Unix(),
+		"iat":   time.Now().Unix(),
+	}
+	tok := jwt.NewWithClaims(jwt.SigningMethodRS256, c)
+	tok.Header["kid"] = kid
+	signed, err := tok.SignedString(key)
+	if err != nil {
+		t.Fatalf("signing test token: %v", err)
+	}
+	return signed
+}

LT-40: JWT verification middleware, live router wiring
## Summary

Implements `handoff-03-auth.md` v3/v4's verifier side and wires LT-39's already-built handlers into `cmd/api-service`'s real router, per `refinement/LT-40.md`. This is the story that turns LT-32's issuer split, LT-39's handlers, and LT-34's health endpoint into an actual authenticated, reachable public endpoint.

## What's new

- **`internal/api/jwks.go`** — a minimal RFC 7517 JWKS client: parses RSA public keys, caches the fetched key set with a TTL matching the token TTL's upper bound (per v4's guidance). A stale-but-present cache survives a transient fetch failure rather than hard-failing every request. Supports a `kid` rotation overlap window by construction (caches the whole published key set, not just the newest key).
- **`internal/api/jwtmiddleware.go`** — the verifier, using `golang-jwt/jwt/v5`:
  - Algorithm pinned to RS256 via `jwt.WithValidMethods` on every parse, never left to the library default. Tested against both RFC 8725 §3.1 attacks named in the spec: a bare `alg:none` token, and an HS256 token forged using the RSA public key's DER bytes as the HMAC secret — both rejected before signature verification is attempted.
  - Public-key-only verification, structurally: nothing in `internal/api` or the verifying binary's path ever references signing-key material.
  - `kid`-based key selection with a real rotation-overlap test (two tokens, two `kid`s, one `KeySource` configured with both, both verify).
  - `iss`/`aud`/`exp`/`sub` checked explicitly; `scope` claim disambiguation: a token naming zero or more than one recognized scope is rejected outright rather than silently picking one (matches this project's standing rule against invented authorization ambiguity — `AuthContext` carries exactly one `Scope` by LT-39's own design).
  - Verification failure returns 401 via the existing `writeUnauthorized` — the actual integration seam with LT-39, not new response-shape design.
  - `NewDeadlineMiddleware` applies a concrete `deadlineBudget` (5s) to every request — the number `decisions/context-propagation.md`'s ruling required, composed outermost so it covers JWT verification (including a JWKS cache-miss fetch) and the handler's DAO call under one budget.
- **`internal/api/stopgap_authorizer.go`** — `StopgapAuthorizer`, a **temporary default-deny** `Authorizer`, per Marcus Ilori's (02) direct ruling after being asked before this was wired to a live URL: a permissive allow-all stand-in must never be deployed to a live public URL. Hard-coded `(sub -> allowed record id)` map, seeded via config for LT-51's QA credential; empty by default (denies everything).
- **`internal/api/grantlimiter.go`** — `GrantLimiter`, the token-issuing endpoint's own failed-grant rate limiting and progressive backoff per `client_id` **and** per source IP independently, with alerting on a sustained failure run against one `client_id` (once per threshold crossing). Genuinely separate from the resource-API `RateLimiter`. Not wired to a live endpoint here (real token issuance is LT-51's scope) — built and tested now so LT-51 doesn't also have to design this under its own deadline.
- **`internal/app/verifier_router.go`** — `NewVerifierRouter` builds the real authenticated router for `ModeVerifier`: `/healthz` plus `POST /profiles/search` and `GET /profiles/{id}` behind `NewDeadlineMiddleware(NewJWTMiddleware(...))`, wired to real `RateLimiter`/`TouchCounter` (2,000 distinct records per rolling 24h, per Marcus's ruling on the number `handoff-03-auth.md` left as "mechanism is your call") and `StdoutAuditLogger`. `cmd/api-service/main.go` now opens a real `dao.Repository` in verifier mode.
- **`internal/config`**: `AUTH_JWKS_URL` (the verifier's only path to public key material) plus the two `StopgapAuthorizer` seed vars.

## Verification

`TestNewVerifierRouter_AuthenticatedSearch_ReachesHandler` proves the whole stack end-to-end, not just piece by piece: signs a real RS256 token, serves a real JWKS document over `httptest`, and confirms a request makes it through deadline middleware, JWT verification, and LT-39's handler to a 200 — the audit log line fires as part of that same request.

`go build`/`go vet` clean. `go test ./... -race` green: 20 new JWT/JWKS tests, 8 new `GrantLimiter` tests, 3 new router-wiring tests, full existing suite unaffected. `gofmt -l` clean.

## What's explicitly not here (per refinement/LT-40.md's non-goals)

Real token issuance logic (`/auth/token`'s actual grant handling) — that's LT-51, sequenced right after this. The real `caller_referral`-backed `Authorizer` — still a stated seam, real implementation is a later story per `handoff-03-auth.md` v3's F8 resolution.

## Live-URL review

Per the refinement doc: LT-40 merges and deploys on its own (verified by the unit/integration tests here), but its live-URL review runs **jointly with LT-51's**, using a real token obtained from `/auth/token` via a dedicated QA client credential — not an out-of-band token.

- **Renata Cole** (03 Engineering & Delivery, lead) — implementation and tests.
- **Naomi Voss** (01, PO) — refinement, corrected `backlog.md`'s stale rate-limit criterion against LT-39's actual merged shape.
- **Desmond Okafor** (01, claims check) — verified the narrowing against PR #26's real diff, caught two doc-status errors.
- **Tobias Lindqvist** (01, objection) — found the missing token-issuance story (now LT-51), routed to team-lead.
- **Marcus Ilori** (02) — ruled the `StopgapAuthorizer` shape and the touch-cap number (2,000/24h) on direct request before this was wired to a live URL.

{
  "command": "cat > /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-warrenthompson-Source-LoginID-02-ai-security-architecture/24287bc9-e284-45c4-9fca-81a6a4212461/scratchpad/pr30-review.md << 'EOF'\nIndependent review: Tomasz Wrede (02 Adversary) — PR #30 (LT-40, JWT verification middleware)\n\nI read the full diff (`gh pr diff 30`), not just the PR description, and checked each of the five things I was asked to attack. Ranked by what I'd fix first.\n\n**1. JWKS fail-open on fetch failure has no staleness bound. HIGH.**\n`JWKSCache.KeyForKid` (`internal/api/jwks.go`): on a failed refresh it keeps serving the last-successfully-fetched key set (`if c.keys == nil { return nil, err }` — else it falls through to the old map), and retries on every subsequent call since `fetchedAt` is never advanced on failure. That retry-every-call behavior is good — it heals fast once the issuer recovers — but there is no ceiling on how long \"stale\" is allowed to get, and no alerting on a prolonged fetch-failure run. Concretely: if a key is being retired *because it's compromised* and the issuer's JWKS endpoint is unreachable during that incident (network partition, or the issuer itself is what's being remediated), the verifier keeps accepting tokens signed by the revoked key indefinitely, for as long as the fetch keeps failing — which is exactly the scenario a bounded staleness window exists to prevent. The code comment reasons about *accepting a new key slightly late* being safe; it doesn't reason about *never stopping accepting an old key* being unsafe. Ask: a max-staleness bound (fail closed — reject all tokens — once the cache has been stale beyond N× the fetch TTL) plus an alert on sustained fetch failure.\n\n**2. Unknown `kid` never forces an out-of-band refetch. MEDIUM-HIGH, and it compounds with #1 in the wrong direction.**\nSame function: the refresh condition is `c.keys == nil || time.Since(c.fetchedAt) >= c.ttl` — it does **not** check \"is this specific kid missing.\" If the issuer rotates in a new key and a client is issued a token under it before the verifier's next scheduled TTL refresh (up to 15 minutes, per `jwksCacheTTL`), that legitimately-signed token gets rejected with \"no key found for kid\" until the cache naturally expires — the code never tries the obvious fix (refetch once on an unrecognized kid, then fail only if it's still missing). Combined with #1, this design fails open on the wrong side (revoked keys linger past their retirement) and fails closed on the wrong side (freshly-rotated keys aren't recognized promptly) — both directions of the rotation-overlap window are handled worse than the design intends. `TestJWTMiddleware_KidRotation_BothKeysVerify` only proves both keys work when both are *already* in one JWKS response fetched once; it doesn't test the timing gap between rotation and refetch.\n\n**3. `StopgapAuthorizer`'s deny path has zero test coverage. MEDIUM.**\nThe default-deny logic itself reads correctly (`ReadOwnAllow[sub]` miss → `false`; unrecognized scope → `false`), and the PM's ruling to require default-deny is followed in the code. But there is no `stopgap_authorizer_test.go` at all in this PR, and `verifier_router_test.go` only exercises `POST /profiles/search` (no per-record authz) plus the unauthenticated and healthz paths — `GET /profiles/{id}` under `read:own` is never hit by a test, allow or deny. \"Confirm the deny path is actually exercised by a test\" — it isn't. The code is probably right; nothing here proves it, and this is exactly the kind of authz code where \"probably right, never tested\" is the wrong bar for something Marcus explicitly flagged as risky enough to rule on before it touched a live URL.\n\n**4. `GrantLimiter` fail-closed-on-store-outage — not yet answerable, flagging forward rather than as a defect.**\nCorrectly keyed independently on `client_id` and source IP (`TestGrantLimiter_KeyedIndependentlyByClientIDAndSourceIP` proves either key blocks the attempt), and it's genuinely not wired to a live endpoint in this PR — the in-process map has no external store to fail. The question only becomes real when LT-51 gives it a shared/distributed backing store; flag now so LT-51 doesn't build that store and discover the fail-open/fail-closed question was never decided.\n\n**5. Algorithm confusion — confirmed, and confirmed properly. PASS, no gap.**\nThis is the finding I raised at S8 as a design-doc gap. It's closed for real here, not just claimed: `jwt.WithValidMethods([]string{jwtAlgorithm})` (RS256-only) is passed on every parse, ahead of the keyfunc — and the test suite has both RFC 8725 §3.1 attacks, not just the easy one: `TestJWTMiddleware_AlgNone_401` (bare `alg:none`) **and** `TestJWTMiddleware_HMACConfusionWithPublicKeyAsSecret_401`, which actually forges an HS256 token using the RSA public key's DER bytes as the HMAC secret and confirms it's rejected before signature verification. That's the specific attack I asked for by name, not a weaker stand-in for it — good work, no notes.\n\nNet: 1 and 2 together are the same underlying gap (no forced refetch tied to key state, only to a calendar TTL) manifesting in both directions of a rotation event; I'd want at least a bounded max-staleness fail-closed added for #1 before this is load-bearing beyond the QA review, since #3 doesn't have that safety net either.\nEOF\nwc -l /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-warrenthompson-Source-LoginID-02-ai-security-architecture/24287bc9-e284-45c4-9fca-81a6a4212461/scratchpad/pr30-review.md"

+	// refresh() failure here has already been handled (logged / staleness
+	// tracked) inside refresh() itself.
+	if c.reserveUnknownKidRetry() {
+		_ = c.refresh(ctx)
+		if key, ok := c.lookupKey(kid); ok {
+			return key, nil
+		}
 	}
-	return key, nil
+
+	return nil, fmt.Errorf("api: no key found for kid %q", kid)
 }
 
-func (c *JWKSCache) fetch() (map[string]*rsa.PublicKey, error) {
-	resp, err := c.httpClient.Get(c.url)
+func (c *JWKSCache) lookupKey(kid string) (*rsa.PublicKey, bool) {
+	c.mu.Lock()
+	defer c.mu.Unlock()
+	k, ok := c.keys[kid]
+	return k, ok
+}
+
+// reserveUnknownKidRetry reports whether an unknown-kid-triggered refetch
+// may proceed now, and if so atomically marks one as just having started
+// — a caller enumerating unknown kids can trigger at most one extra
+// fetch per jwksUnknownKidRefetchInterval, not one per request.
+func (c *JWKSCache) reserveUnknownKidRetry() bool {
+	c.mu.Lock()
+	defer c.mu.Unlock()
+	if c.now().Sub(c.lastUnknownKidRetry) < jwksUnknownKidRefetchInterval {
+		return false
+	}
+	c.lastUnknownKidRetry = c.now()
+	return true
+}
+
+// refresh performs the network fetch (no lock held during it) and
+// updates cache state. Returns an error only when the caller must fail
+// the whole verification: no cache exists yet (cold start against an
+// unreachable issuer), or the existing cache has exceeded maxStaleness —
+// wrapped in ErrJWKSStale in the latter case specifically, so the
+// middleware can log AuditJWKSUnavailable rather than an ordinary auth
+// failure. A transient failure within the staleness bound returns nil:
+// the stale cache is still good enough to use, per the resilience this
+// mechanism exists for.
+func (c *JWKSCache) refresh(ctx context.Context) error {
+	fresh, err := c.fetch(ctx)
+
+	c.mu.Lock()
+	defer c.mu.Unlock()
+
+	if err == nil {
+		c.keys = fresh
+		c.fetchedAt = c.now()
+		c.consecutiveFailures = 0
+		c.staleAlerted = false
+		return nil
+	}
+
+	c.consecutiveFailures++
+
+	if c.keys == nil {
+		return err
+	}
+
+	staleSince := c.now().Sub(c.fetchedAt)
+	if staleSince > c.maxStaleness {
+		if !c.staleAlerted {
+			c.staleAlerted = true
+			// Log line for 04's alerting pipeline on a prolonged
+			// fetch-failure run — not a policy decision (AuditLogger is
+			// for those), an operational signal that this process's own
+			// key infrastructure is degraded.
+			log.Printf("api: JWKS unrefreshable for %s across %d consecutive failures (exceeds max staleness %s) — failing closed: %v",
+				staleSince, c.consecutiveFailures, c.maxStaleness, err)
+		}
+		return fmt.Errorf("%w: stale for %s across %d consecutive failures: %v", ErrJWKSStale, staleSince, c.consecutiveFailures, err)
+	}
+	return nil
+}
+
+func (c *JWKSCache) fetch(ctx context.Context) (map[string]*rsa.PublicKey, error) {
+	req, err := http.NewRequestWithContext(ctx, http.MethodGet, c.url, nil)
+	if err != nil {
+		return nil, fmt.Errorf("api: building JWKS request for %s: %w", c.url, err)
+	}
+	resp, err := c.httpClient.Do(req)
 	if err != nil {
 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("api: fetching JWKS from %s: %w", c.url, err)
 	}

+// jwksTestServer serves body while up, or a configurable failure while

+func newJWKSTestServer(body []byte) *jwksTestServer {

+// TestJWKSCache_FailsClosedPastMaxStaleness is the PM's ruling on Tomasz

+// compromised, and if the JWKS endpoint is unreachable during that same

+// TestStopgapAuthorizer_ReadOwn_AllowedAndDenied is Marcus Ilori's (02)
+// ruling on the review finding that this stand-in's deny path had no
+// test coverage: exercise both the allow and deny outcomes for
+// ScopeReadOwn, the only scope this stopgap actually gates.
+func TestStopgapAuthorizer_ReadOwn_AllowedAndDenied(t *testing.T) {
+	a := &StopgapAuthorizer{
+		ReadOwnAllow: map[string]map[string]bool{
+			"qa-client": {"profile-1": true},
+		},
+	}
+	ctx := context.Background()
+
+	allowed, err := a.Authorize(ctx, "qa-client", ScopeReadOwn, "profile-1")
+	if err != nil {
+		t.Fatalf("Authorize: %v", err)
+	}
+	if !allowed {
+		t.Error("qa-client reading its seeded profile-1 should be allowed")
+	}
+
+	denied, err := a.Authorize(ctx, "qa-client", ScopeReadOwn, "profile-2")
+	if err != nil {
+		t.Fatalf("Authorize: %v", err)
+	}
+	if denied {
+		t.Error("qa-client reading a profile it was not seeded with should be denied")
+	}
+
+	deniedSub, err := a.Authorize(ctx, "some-other-client", ScopeReadOwn, "profile-1")
+	if err != nil {
+		t.Fatalf("Authorize: %v", err)
+	}
+	if deniedSub {
+		t.Error("a sub with no seeded entry at all should be denied, the safe default")
+	}
+}

+// TestStopgapAuthorizer_EmptyMapDeniesEverything is the safe-default
+// requirement itself: a StopgapAuthorizer constructed with no seed data
+// (the out-of-the-box state before LT-51 seeds a QA client credential)
+// must deny every ScopeReadOwn request, never fail open.
+func TestStopgapAuthorizer_EmptyMapDeniesEverything(t *testing.T) {
+	a := &StopgapAuthorizer{}

+		t.Fatalf("Authorize: %v", err)
+	}
+	if allowed {
+		t.Error("an unseeded StopgapAuthorizer must deny every read:own request, not fail open")
+	}

+func TestStopgapAuthorizer_ReadAnyAndSearch_AlwaysAllowed(t *testing.T) {

No notes.**\n`maxStaleness = 3 * ttl`, `refresh()` fails closed with `ErrJWKSStale` once exceeded, distinct `AuditJWKSUnavailable` event plus a log line for 04's alerting, and `TestJWKSCache_FailsClosedPastMaxStaleness` actually drives the clock past the bound and confirms the error wraps `ErrJWKSStale`. No notes.**\n`stopgap_authorizer_test.go` covers allow, deny-by-record, deny-by-unknown-sub, and the empty-map safe-default; `TestNewVerifierRouter_ReadOwn_AllowedAndDenied` drives it through the real router with the real config wiring, both 200 and 403. That means the 30s rate limit is consumed by *any* unknown-kid lookup, not specifically by the legitimate one. An unauthenticated caller can send a bearer token with a syntactically valid JWT and a garbage `kid` — no valid signature required to reach the keyfunc, since `KeyForKid` is called before signature verification — and that alone claims the rate-limit slot. Timed to repeat roughly every 30s (trivial, unauthenticated, costs nothing), an attacker can keep the slot permanently occupied by noise, so the *actual* freshly-rotated key's first legitimate lookup arrives during an already-consumed window, gets no refetch, and falls back to waiting for the next calendar TTL rollover — which is most of finding #2's original ~15-minute gap, just requiring an attacker to bother.\n\nTo be fair to the fix: this is strictly better than before (an attacker has to actively maintain the interference; a quiet rotation with no attacker present now recovers in ≤30s instead of ≤15min), and rate-limiting *something* here is correct — the alternative of refetching on every unknown kid reopens the exact hammering concern the limit exists for. The gap is specifically that the gate isn't scoped to \"this kid, once\" — it's scoped to \"any kid, globally.\" A fix in the direction of a small per-kid dedup (e.g., only reserve/consume the slot for a kid not already attempted in the current window, or key the rate limit by kid with a short per-kid TTL rather than one global timestamp) would close this without reopening the flood risk. Not blocking on its own given the bound is now 30s instead of unbounded-until-TTL, but I'd want it named as an accepted residual explicitly if it's not going to be fixed now, rather than left implicit.\n\nNet: 1 and 3 are genuinely done. 2 is real progress with a residual gap in the mechanism it added, not a failure to attempt the fix.\nEOF\ngh pr comment 30 --repo wtvamp/loginid-takehome --body-file /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-warrenthompson-Source-LoginID-02-ai-security-architecture/24287bc9-e284-45c4-9fca-81a6a4212461/scratchpad/pr30-recheck.md"

Posted: https://github.com/wtvamp/loginid-takehome/pull/30#issuecomment-5653074168 Same text for you to relay (no working SendMessage this session, as before): --- **Independent review: Tomasz Wrede (02 Adversary) — PR #30 re-check at `03181f6`** **Finding 1 (JWKS fail-open) — CLOSED, no notes.** `maxStaleness = 3×ttl`, fails closed with `ErrJWKSStale`, distinct `AuditJWKSUnavailable` event, and `TestJWKSCache_FailsClosedPastMaxStaleness` actually drives the clock past the bound and confirms it. An unauthenticated caller can send a bearer token with a garbage `kid` (no valid signature needed — `KeyForKid` runs before signature check) and consume the 30s slot for free. Timed to repeat every 30s, an attacker can permanently occupy the slot with noise, so a legitimately-rotated key's real lookup arrives during an already-consumed window and falls back to the full calendar-TTL rollover — recreating most of the original ~15-minute gap, just requiring an attacker to bother maintaining it. To be fair: strictly better than before — a quiet rotation with no attacker present now recovers in ≤30s instead of ≤15min — and rate-limiting *something* here is correct, since refetching on every unknown kid reopens the hammering concern the limit exists to prevent. The fix needed is scope, not removal: key the reservation per-`kid` (or dedup within the window) rather than one global timestamp. Not blocking given the bound is now 30s worst-case instead of unbounded, but I'd want it named as an explicit accepted residual if it's not getting fixed now. Net: 1 and 3 done, 2 is real progress with a residual gap in the mechanism it added.

 	jwksMaxStalenessMultiple      = 3
 	jwksUnknownKidRefetchInterval = 30 * time.Second
+
+	// jwksMinRefreshRetryInterval floors how often a TTL-expired cache
+	// with a just-failed refresh will attempt another real network
+	// fetch. Without this, every single request during a sustained
+	// issuer outage triggers its own fetch attempt for as long as the
+	// outage lasts (up to maxStaleness) — the same "don't hammer a
+	// struggling dependency" reasoning already applied to the
+	// unknown-kid path, missing from the ordinary refresh path (Oren
+	// Castellan, PR #30 re-review). Short relative to ttl/maxStaleness:
+	// this only throttles retries during an active failure, it must not
+	// delay picking up a healthy issuer again once it recovers.
+	jwksMinRefreshRetryInterval = 5 * time.Second
+
+	// jwksMaxTrackedUnknownKids bounds how many distinct kids can hold an
+	// outstanding unknown-kid retry reservation at once. Without this,
+	// keying the reservation per-kid (see reserveUnknownKidRetry) reopens
+	// its own version of the same map-growth concern a flood of DISTINCT
+	// garbage kids would create: capped here, so an attacker sending many
+	// different fabricated kids can force at most this many extra
+	// fetches per jwksUnknownKidRefetchInterval, not one per distinct
+	// value they bother to send.

+// may proceed now for THIS specific kid, and if so atomically marks one
+// as just having started. Keyed per kid, not by one global timestamp
+// (Ingrid Solano, 02 cold re-check): KeyForKid runs before signature
+// verification, so an unauthenticated caller can send a token with a
+// fabricated kid at no cost — a single global gate lets that caller
+// permanently occupy the one reservation slot by repeating it every
+// jwksUnknownKidRefetchInterval, pushing a legitimately-rotated real
+// key's own refetch back to the full calendar-TTL rollover, silently
+// recreating most of the original gap this mechanism exists to close.
+// Per-kid closes that: a garbage kid's repeated reservation never blocks
+// a DIFFERENT (real) kid's own reservation. jwksMaxTrackedUnknownKids
+// caps how many distinct kids can hold a reservation at once, so a flood
+// of distinct fabricated kids still can't hammer the issuer past that
+// bound — rate-limiting is scoped per-kid, not removed.
+func (c *JWKSCache) reserveUnknownKidRetry(kid string) bool {

{
  "command": "cat > /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-warrenthompson-Source-LoginID-02-ai-security-architecture/24287bc9-e284-45c4-9fca-81a6a4212461/scratchpad/pr30-recheck2.md << 'EOF'\nIndependent review: Tomasz Wrede (02 Adversary) — PR #30 re-check at `a615dfa`\n\n**The griefing path I raised is closed. Per-kid keying does what it's supposed to.**\n`reserveUnknownKidRetry(kid)` now checks/sets `unknownKidRetryAt[kid]`, so an attacker occupying a garbage kid's own 30s window has no effect on a *different* kid's reservation — confirmed by `TestJWKSCache_UnknownKidReservationIsPerKidNotGlobal`, which reproduces exactly the scenario I described (attacker holds `garbage-kid-from-attacker` hot, real `key-new` still gets its reservation and succeeds in the same window). Good, targeted fix — no notes.\n\n**The 64-kid cap: answering your two questions directly.**\n\n1. *Can an attacker exhaust the cap with 64+ distinct garbage kids?* Yes, trivially — `KeyForKid` runs before signature verification, so a garbage kid costs an attacker nothing but a syntactically-valid JWT shell, and there's no request-volume throttle on this path pre-auth (the resource-API `RateLimiter` only applies to an already-authenticated `sub`). `TestJWKSCache_UnknownKidReservationCapsDistinctKids` confirms the cap holds (`jwksMaxTrackedUnknownKids+20` distinct kids trigger at most 64 extra fetches) — but that test only checks the issuer-side fetch count stays bounded, it doesn't check what happens to a *real* kid that shows up as the unlucky 65th while an attacker is actively keeping 64 slots full. That's the scenario worth naming.\n\n2. *What happens to the 65th kid — unrestricted refetch, or rejection?* Rejection, and that's the right direction. `reserveUnknownKidRetry` denies the reservation once `len(unknownKidRetryAt) >= jwksMaxTrackedUnknownKids` (after sweeping expired entries), `KeyForKid` then falls straight to `\"no key found for kid\"` with no fetch attempted — it does **not** fall through to an unrestricted/unconditional refetch. So the issuer itself stays protected regardless of how hard an attacker floods this: worst case is a real key's early lookup gets no out-of-band refetch and waits for the ordinary calendar-TTL rollover, not that the cap gets bypassed and the issuer gets hammered anyway. That was the important direction to get right, and it's right.\n\n**Net assessment: this is a real, substantial narrowing, not a full close — and I think that's the correct place to stop, not a gap to keep chasing.**\nBefore `03181f6`: any single unauthenticated request with a garbage kid could starve *every* real kid, indefinitely, for free. After `a615dfa`: an attacker needs to sustain **≥64 concurrently-live distinct garbage-kid reservations** to starve one additional real kid past the cap, and even then the failure mode is \"wait up to the full TTL,\" not \"issuer gets hammered\" or \"verification silently opens up.\" That's a meaningfully higher cost attack (sustained volume, not one request) for a bounded, fail-safe-direction outcome. I'd call the residual risk LOW now, versus HIGH originally — closing it further would mean either raising the cap (doesn't fix the shape, just the number) or adding per-source-IP/per-caller throttling ahead of this whole path (a real fix, but a bigger scope change than this story's — that's the resource-API's rate-limit story's territory, not this cache's). I'd take this as done for LT-40 and let the pre-auth request-volume question be a named follow-up if 04/03 want one, rather than block here.\nEOF\ngh pr comment 30 --repo wtvamp/loginid-takehome --body-file /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-warrenthompson-Source-LoginID-02-ai-security-architecture/24287bc9-e284-45c4-9fca-81a6a4212461/scratchpad/pr30-recheck2.md"

976	+	// request object is never retained past this function returning.
977	+	req, err := http.NewRequestWithContext(ctx, http.MethodPost, c.baseURL+"/auth", bytes.NewReader(reqBody))
978	+	if err != nil {
979	+		return "", fmt.Errorf("connector: building vendor auth request: %w", err)
980	+	}
981	+	req.Header.Set("Content-Type", "application/json")
982	+
983	+	resp, err := c.httpClient.Do(req)
984	+	if err != nil {
985	+		// Never wrap or surface the raw error to our own caller —
986	+		// connector-security.md §4: vendor errors must not distinguish
987	+		// which part of the credential was wrong, and a raw dial/DNS
988	+		// error can itself carry sensitive detail (host, IP). The
989	+		// caller-facing generic failure is this package's caller's job
990	+		// (internal/connector's handlers); this method's contract is
991	+		// simply "success or ErrVendorAuthFailed", nothing richer.
992	+		return "", ErrVendorAuthFailed
993	+	}
994	+	defer func() { _, _ = io.Copy(io.Discard, resp.Body); _ = resp.Body.Close() }()
995	+
996	+	if resp.StatusCode != http.StatusOK {
997	+		// Do not read/log the response body — connector-security.md §3:
998	+		// "do not log raw vendor response bodies by default, since a
999	+		// vendor error payload can itself echo back submitted PII or
1000	+		// partial credentials."
1001	+		return "", ErrVendorAuthFailed
1002	+	}
1003	+
1004	+	var body vendorAuthResponse
1005	+	if err := json.NewDecoder(io.LimitReader(resp.Body, 1<<20)).Decode(&body); err != nil || body.AccessToken == "" {
1006	+		return "", ErrVendorAuthFailed
1007	+	}
1008	+	return body.AccessToken, nil
1009	+}
1010	+
1011	+type vendorIdentityRequest struct {
1012	+	Phone string `json:"phone"`
1013	+	Name  string `json:"name"`
1014	+}
1015	+
1016	+type vendorIdentityResponse struct {
1017	+	Name          string `json:"name"`
1018	+	Phone         string `json:"phone"`
1019	+	StreetAddress string `json:"street_address"`
1020	+	Locality      string `json:"locality"`
1021	+	Region        string `json:"region"`
1022	+	PostalCode    string `json:"postal_code"`
1023	+	Country       string `json:"country"`
1024	+}
1025	+
1026	+func (c *HTTPVendorClient) FetchIdentity(ctx context.Context, accessToken, phone, name string) (Identity, error) {
1027	+	reqBody, err := json.Marshal(vendorIdentityRequest{Phone: phone, Name: name})
1028	+	if err != nil {
1029	+		return Identity{}, fmt.Errorf("connector: encoding vendor identity request: %w", err)
1030	+	}
1031	+
1032	+	req, err := http.NewRequestWithContext(ctx, http.MethodPost, c.baseURL+"/identity", bytes.NewReader(reqBody))
1033	+	if err != nil {
1034	+		return Identity{}, fmt.Errorf("connector: building vendor identity request: %w", err)
1035	+	}
1036	+	req.Header.Set("Content-Type", "application/json")
1037	+	// The one place accessToken is used — set on this request only,
1038	+	// never on a client-level default header, never retained after this
1039	+	// function returns.
1040	+	req.Header.Set("Authorization", "Bearer "+accessToken)
1041	+
1042	+	resp, err := c.httpClient.Do(req)
1043	+	if err != nil {
1044	+		return Identity{}, ErrVendorIdentityNotFound
1045	+	}
1046	+	defer func() { _, _ = io.Copy(io.Discard, resp.Body); _ = resp.Body.Close() }()
1047	+
1048	+	if resp.StatusCode != http.StatusOK {
1049	+		return Identity{}, ErrVendorIdentityNotFound
1050	+	}
1051	+
1052	+	var body vendorIdentityResponse
1053	+	if err := json.NewDecoder(io.LimitReader(resp.Body, 1<<20)).Decode(&body); err != nil {
1054	+		return Identity{}, ErrVendorIdentityNotFound
1055	+	}
1056	+	return Identity{
1057	+		Name:          body.Name,
1058	+		Phone:         body.Phone,
1059	+		StreetAddress: body.StreetAddress,
1060	+		Locality:      body.Locality,
1061	+		Region:        body.Region,
1062	+		PostalCode:    body.PostalCode,
1063	+		Country:       body.Country,
1064	+	}, nil
1065	+}

1093	+// TestHTTPVendorClient_FetchIdentity_FreshAuthorizationHeaderPerRequest
1094	+// is attack-tree leaf 1's own named enforcement site (refinement/LT-41.md):
1095	+// two sequential calls through the SAME pooled *http.Client, each
1096	+// presenting a DIFFERENT vendor token, must each produce an outbound
1097	+// Authorization header matching ITS OWN token — never the previous
1098	+// call's, which a client that held a shared/default header (instead of
1099	+// building it fresh per call) could otherwise leak across requests via
1100	+// connection reuse.
1101	+func TestHTTPVendorClient_FetchIdentity_FreshAuthorizationHeaderPerRequest(t *testing.T) {
1102	+	var mu sync.Mutex
1103	+	var observedHeaders []string
1104	+
1105	+	srv := httptest.NewServer(http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
1106	+		mu.Lock()
1107	+		observedHeaders = append(observedHeaders, r.Header.Get("Authorization"))
1108	+		mu.Unlock()
1109	+		w.Header().Set("Content-Type", "application/json")
1110	+		_ = json.NewEncoder(w).Encode(vendorIdentityResponse{Name: "ok"})
1111	+	}))
1112	+	defer srv.Close()
1113	+
1114	+	// Construct directly (bypassing NewHTTPVendorClient's https-only
1115	+	// check, which httptest.Server's plain-http URL would otherwise
1116	+	// fail) — this file is in the same package, so it can set the
1117	+	// unexported fields directly rather than needing a second,
1118	+	// test-only constructor in non-test code.
1119	+	client := &HTTPVendorClient{baseURL: srv.URL, httpClient: srv.Client()}
1120	+
1121	+	if _, err := client.FetchIdentity(context.Background(), "token-A", "+15555550100", "Jane"); err != nil {
1122	+		t.Fatalf("first FetchIdentity call: %v", err)
1123	+	}
1124	+	if _, err := client.FetchIdentity(context.Background(), "token-B", "+15555550100", "Jane"); err != nil {
1125	+		t.Fatalf("second FetchIdentity call: %v", err)
1126	+	}
1127	+
1128	+	if len(observedHeaders) != 2 {
1129	+		t.Fatalf("server observed %d requests, want 2", len(observedHeaders))
1130	+	}
1131	+	if observedHeaders[0] != "Bearer token-A" {
1132	+		t.Errorf("first request's Authorization = %q, want %q", observedHeaders[0], "Bearer token-A")
1133	+	}
1134	+	if observedHeaders[1] != "Bearer token-B" {
1135	+		t.Errorf("second request's Authorization = %q, want %q — must not inherit the first call's token via connection reuse", observedHeaders[1], "Bearer token-B")
1136	+	}
1137	+}

123	+// connectorJWKSCacheTTL matches api-service's own verifier JWKS TTL
124	+// (internal/app/verifier_router.go) — both binaries verify tokens minted
125	+// by the same issuer, so there's no reason for a different refresh
126	+// cadence.
127	+const connectorJWKSCacheTTL = 15 * time.Minute
128	+
129	+// connectorVendorLimiterBaseBackoff/MaxBackoff/AlertThreshold mirror
130	+// NewIssuerRouter's own GrantLimiter constants — same reasoning
131	+// (handoff-03-auth.md names the mechanism, not specific numbers), reused
132	+// here for the vendor-call rate limiter per LT-41's attack-tree leaf 5
133	+// control rather than inventing a second set of magic numbers.
134	+const (
135	+	connectorVendorLimiterBaseBackoff    = 1 * time.Second
136	+	connectorVendorLimiterMaxBackoff     = 5 * time.Minute
137	+	connectorVendorLimiterAlertThreshold = 5
138	+)
139	+
140	+// NewConnectorRouter builds cmd/idp-connector's real router — LT-41's
141	+// /auth and /identity handlers behind the connector's own inbound JWT
142	+// verification (a distinct audience, connector-security.md §5: aud =
143	+// cfg.ConnectorJWTAudience, scope = connector:identity-lookup — NOT
144	+// api-service's audience, so a profile:*-scoped api-service token
145	+// cannot be replayed against this connector and vice versa).
146	+//
147	+// vendor is the outbound seam to the third-party IDP — either a real
148	+// (HTTPVendorClient) or stub (StubVendorClient) implementation;
149	+// cmd/idp-connector/main.go decides which based on whether
150	+// IDP_ABC_BASE_URL is configured.
151	+func NewConnectorRouter(cfg config.Config, keys api.KeySource, vendor connector.VendorClient) http.Handler {
152	+	mux := http.NewServeMux()
153	+	mux.HandleFunc("/healthz", healthHandler("idp-connector"))
154	+
155	+	auditLog := api.StdoutAuditLogger{}
156	+	authMiddleware := api.NewJWTMiddleware(keys, cfg.AuthJWTIssuer, cfg.ConnectorJWTAudience, auditLog)
157	+	protect := func(h http.Handler) http.Handler {
158	+		return api.NewDeadlineMiddleware(authMiddleware(h))
159	+	}
160	+
161	+	// One shared limiter instance across both handlers — a caller
162	+	// hammering /auth and a caller hammering /identity are the same
163	+	// abuse signal against the same vendor, keyed by the same
164	+	// (calling-API-client sub, source IP) pair (LT-41 attack-tree
165	+	// leaf 5). Reuses api.GrantLimiter (PR #30/#34) rather than
166	+	// building a new rate-limit type, per this story's own non-goals.
167	+	limiter := api.NewInProcessGrantLimiter(
168	+		connectorVendorLimiterBaseBackoff, connectorVendorLimiterMaxBackoff, connectorVendorLimiterAlertThreshold,
169	+		func(clientID string, consecutiveFailures int) {
170	+			log.Printf("idp-connector: ALERT sustained failed vendor calls from caller sub=%s (%d consecutive)", clientID, consecutiveFailures)
171	+		},
172	+	)
173	+
174	+	deps := connector.Deps{Vendor: vendor, Limiter: limiter, AuditLog: auditLog}
175	+	mux.Handle("POST /auth", protect(connector.NewAuthHandler(deps)))
176	+	mux.Handle("POST /identity", protect(connector.NewIdentityHandler(deps)))
177	+
178	+	return mux
179	+}

372	+package connector
373	+
374	+import (
375	+	"context"
376	+	"encoding/json"
377	+	"io"
378	+	"net"
379	+	"net/http"
380	+	"strings"
381	+	"time"
382	+
383	+	"loginid-takehome/internal/api"
384	+)
385	+
386	+// identityFailureFloor is /identity's uniform response-time floor across
387	+// its three failure branches (no-token, bad-token, vendor-rejected) —
388	+// Marcus Ilori's (02) ruling on Tobias Lindqvist's timing-oracle
389	+// objection (refinement/LT-41.md), recorded in full in
390	+// connector-security.md §4. ~100ms, well under any context.WithTimeout
391	+// budget elsewhere in the chain (vendorCallTimeout is 10s) so it never
392	+// competes with those deadlines. This narrows, but does not fully close,
393	+// the gap between a local-only failure (microseconds) and a
394	+// vendor-round-trip failure (tens-hundreds of ms) — full closure is an
395	+// accepted non-goal per that ruling.
396	+const identityFailureFloor = 100 * time.Millisecond
397	+
398	+// identityErrorBody is the one JSON shape every /identity failure
399	+// returns, regardless of which of the three branches produced it —
400	+// content-identical by construction (a single writeIdentityFailure call
401	+// site below), not by three call sites happening to independently agree.
402	+type identityErrorBody struct {
403	+	Error string `json:"error"`
404	+}
405	+
406	+// vendorLimiter is the interface these handlers depend on — satisfied
407	+// directly by api.GrantLimiter (PR #30/#34's already-built component,
408	+// reused here rather than rebuilt per LT-41's own non-goals: "this
409	+// story's own in-process rate limiting is sufficient, matching the
410	+// pattern already used for RateLimiter/TouchCounter/GrantLimiter").
411	+// Declared locally so this package's own tests can supply a minimal fake
412	+// satisfying only the calls actually made here, per
413	+// decisions/test-double-strategy.md.
414	+type vendorLimiter interface {
415	+	Allow(ctx context.Context, clientID, sourceIP string) (bool, error)
416	+	RecordFailure(ctx context.Context, clientID, sourceIP string)
417	+	RecordSuccess(ctx context.Context, clientID, sourceIP string)
418	+}
419	+
420	+// Deps bundles NewAuthHandler/NewIdentityHandler's collaborators.
421	+type Deps struct {
422	+	Vendor   VendorClient
423	+	Limiter  vendorLimiter
424	+	AuditLog api.AuditLogger
425	+}
426	+
427	+func sourceIPFrom(r *http.Request) string {
428	+	host, _, err := net.SplitHostPort(r.RemoteAddr)
429	+	if err != nil {
430	+		return r.RemoteAddr
431	+	}
432	+	return host
433	+}
434	+
435	+func writeJSON(w http.ResponseWriter, status int, v any) {
436	+	w.Header().Set("Content-Type", "application/json")
437	+	w.WriteHeader(status)
438	+	_ = json.NewEncoder(w).Encode(v)
439	+}
440	+
441	+// writeIdentityFailure is the single call site for every /identity
442	+// failure response — the one place that guarantees byte-identical
443	+// content across the no-token, bad-token, and vendor-rejected branches
444	+// (connector-security.md §1/F3), never three call sites independently
445	+// trying to agree.
446	+func writeIdentityFailure(w http.ResponseWriter) {
447	+	writeJSON(w, http.StatusUnauthorized, identityErrorBody{Error: "identity_lookup_failed"})
448	+}
449	+
450	+// waitForFloor sleeps out the remainder of identityFailureFloor since
451	+// start, if any — applied uniformly across /identity's three failure
452	+// branches so none of them is distinguishable from the others by
453	+// response latency alone (partial mitigation per connector-security.md
454	+// §4's own accepted-residual language; not applied to the success path,
455	+// which was never part of the timing-oracle concern).
456	+func waitForFloor(start time.Time) {
457	+	if remaining := identityFailureFloor - time.Since(start); remaining > 0 {
458	+		time.Sleep(remaining)
459	+	}
460	+}
461	+
462	+// authRequestBody is the assignment's own fixed POST /auth contract.
463	+type authRequestBody struct {
464	+	Username string `json:"username"`
465	+	Password string `json:"password"`
466	+}
467	+
468	+type authResponseBody struct {
469	+	AccessToken string `json:"access_token"`
470	+}
471	+
472	+// NewAuthHandler implements POST /auth. The caller (in this project's
473	+// shape, cmd/api-service acting on an onboarding flow's behalf) must
474	+// already be authenticated with a connector:identity-lookup-scoped,
475	+// idp-connector-service-audience token — enforced by whatever middleware
476	+// wraps this handler (internal/app.NewConnectorRouter), not by this
477	+// function; AuthContextFromContext here is read-only identification for
478	+// rate-limiting and audit purposes.
479	+func NewAuthHandler(deps Deps) http.HandlerFunc {
480	+	return func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
481	+		ctx := r.Context()
482	+		ac, _ := api.AuthContextFromContext(ctx)
483	+		sourceIP := sourceIPFrom(r)
484	+
485	+		allowed, err := deps.Limiter.Allow(ctx, ac.Sub, sourceIP)
486	+		if err != nil {
487	+			writeJSON(w, http.StatusInternalServerError, identityErrorBody{Error: "server_error"})
488	+			return
489	+		}
490	+		if !allowed {
491	+			writeJSON(w, http.StatusTooManyRequests, identityErrorBody{Error: "slow_down"})
492	+			return
493	+		}
494	+
495	+		var body authRequestBody
496	+		if err := json.NewDecoder(io.LimitReader(r.Body, 1<<16)).Decode(&body); err != nil {
497	+			writeJSON(w, http.StatusBadRequest, identityErrorBody{Error: "invalid_request"})
498	+			return
499	+		}
500	+
501	+		// accessToken is scoped to this block only — read from the
502	+		// vendor call's return value, written straight into the
503	+		// response, never assigned to any wider-scoped variable or
504	+		// logged. This IS the narrowest this value can be scoped given
505	+		// /auth's own contract: its job is delivering the token to the
506	+		// caller, so it necessarily reaches the response writer, but
507	+		// nothing else.
508	+		accessToken, err := deps.Vendor.Authenticate(ctx, body.Username, body.Password)
509	+		if err != nil {
510	+			deps.Limiter.RecordFailure(ctx, ac.Sub, sourceIP)
511	+			logAuditOutcome(ctx, deps.AuditLog, ac.Sub, api.AuditAuthnFailure)
512	+			// Generic failure regardless of *why* the vendor rejected
513	+			// it (unknown username vs. wrong password vs. vendor
514	+			// unreachable) — connector-security.md §4: "must not leak
515	+			// which part of the credential was wrong."
516	+			writeJSON(w, http.StatusUnauthorized, identityErrorBody{Error: "vendor_authentication_failed"})
517	+			return
518	+		}
519	+		deps.Limiter.RecordSuccess(ctx, ac.Sub, sourceIP)
520	+		logAuditOutcome(ctx, deps.AuditLog, ac.Sub, api.AuditSuccess)
521	+
522	+		writeJSON(w, http.StatusOK, authResponseBody{AccessToken: accessToken})
523	+	}
524	+}
525	+
526	+// identityRequestBody is the assignment's own fixed POST /identity
527	+// contract — no credential field, since the vendor token travels as a
528	+// request header instead (connector-security.md §1).
529	+type identityRequestBody struct {
530	+	Phone string `json:"phone"`
531	+	Name  string `json:"name"`
532	+}
533	+
534	+type identityResponseBody struct {
535	+	Name          string `json:"name"`
536	+	Phone         string `json:"phone"`
537	+	StreetAddress string `json:"street_address"`
538	+	Locality      string `json:"locality"`
539	+	Region        string `json:"region"`
540	+	PostalCode    string `json:"postal_code"`
541	+	Country       string `json:"country"`
542	+}
543	+
544	+// vendorAccessTokenHeader is the header /identity accepts the vendor's
545	+// access_token on — a header, not a body field, since the assignment's
546	+// fixed {"phone","name"} body has no credential field (LT-41 acceptance
547	+// criteria).
548	+const vendorAccessTokenHeader = "X-Vendor-Access-Token"
549	+
550	+// NewIdentityHandler implements POST /identity. Every failure path
551	+// (missing token, malformed-looking token, vendor rejection) returns
552	+// through writeIdentityFailure after waitForFloor — the two calls
553	+// together are what make the three branches content- and
554	+// (approximately) time-identical, per connector-security.md §1/§4.
555	+func NewIdentityHandler(deps Deps) http.HandlerFunc {
556	+	return func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
557	+		start := time.Now()
558	+		ctx := r.Context()
559	+		ac, _ := api.AuthContextFromContext(ctx)
560	+		sourceIP := sourceIPFrom(r)
561	+
562	+		allowed, err := deps.Limiter.Allow(ctx, ac.Sub, sourceIP)
563	+		if err != nil {
564	+			writeJSON(w, http.StatusInternalServerError, identityErrorBody{Error: "server_error"})
565	+			return
566	+		}
567	+		if !allowed {
568	+			// Not one of the three timing-oracle branches (it's an
569	+			// overt, openly-disclosed 429, not a covert signal) — no
570	+			// floor applied.
571	+			writeJSON(w, http.StatusTooManyRequests, identityErrorBody{Error: "slow_down"})
572	+			return
573	+		}
574	+
575	+		var body identityRequestBody
576	+		if err := json.NewDecoder(io.LimitReader(r.Body, 1<<16)).Decode(&body); err != nil {
577	+			// A malformed body is a caller bug, not one of the three
578	+			// named credential/token-validity branches — ordinary 400,
579	+			// no floor.
580	+			writeJSON(w, http.StatusBadRequest, identityErrorBody{Error: "invalid_request"})
581	+			return
582	+		}
583	+
584	+		token := strings.TrimSpace(r.Header.Get(vendorAccessTokenHeader))
585	+		if token == "" {
586	+			deps.Limiter.RecordFailure(ctx, ac.Sub, sourceIP)
587	+			logAuditOutcome(ctx, deps.AuditLog, ac.Sub, api.AuditAuthnFailure)
588	+			waitForFloor(start)
589	+			writeIdentityFailure(w)
590	+			return
591	+		}
592	+
593	+		// token is scoped to this function only, passed by value into
594	+		// FetchIdentity, and never assigned to any variable outside
595	+		// this block or returned — attack-tree leaf 4's control
596	+		// (connector-security.md §1/Attack-tree row 4).
597	+		identity, err := deps.Vendor.FetchIdentity(ctx, token, body.Phone, body.Name)
598	+		if err != nil {
599	+			deps.Limiter.RecordFailure(ctx, ac.Sub, sourceIP)
600	+			logAuditOutcome(ctx, deps.AuditLog, ac.Sub, api.AuditAuthnFailure)
601	+			waitForFloor(start)
602	+			writeIdentityFailure(w)
603	+			return
604	+		}
605	+		deps.Limiter.RecordSuccess(ctx, ac.Sub, sourceIP)
606	+		logAuditOutcome(ctx, deps.AuditLog, ac.Sub, api.AuditSuccess)
607	+
608	+		// Success is not part of the timing-oracle concern — no floor.
609	+		writeJSON(w, http.StatusOK, identityResponseBody{
610	+			Name:          identity.Name,
611	+			Phone:         identity.Phone,
612	+			StreetAddress: identity.StreetAddress,
613	+			Locality:      identity.Locality,
614	+			Region:        identity.Region,
615	+			PostalCode:    identity.PostalCode,
616	+			Country:       identity.Country,
617	+		})
618	+	}
619	+}
620	+
621	+// logAuditOutcome logs caller sub/scope/timestamp/outcome only — never
622	+// the vendor username/password, vendor access_token, or any PII field
623	+// value, per connector-security.md §3/§5's audit discipline.
624	+func logAuditOutcome(ctx context.Context, auditLog api.AuditLogger, sub string, kind api.AuditEventKind) {
625	+	if auditLog == nil {
626	+		return
627	+	}
628	+	auditLog.Log(ctx, api.AuditEvent{
629	+		Sub:       sub,
630	+		Scope:     api.ScopeConnectorIdentityLookup,
631	+		Kind:      kind,
632	+		Timestamp: time.Now().UTC(),
633	+	})
634	+}

Bash grep -n "floor\|timing\|100ms\|100 ms\|oracle" /Users/warrenthompson/Source/LoginID/02-ai-security-a
